People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Honestly, the thing that kind of scared me into coming in is my eating has been really all over the place. Like, some days I’ll get through most of the day and realize I just… didn’t eat. Not even on purpose, I just forget meals completely, or I’ll feel like stopping to eat is too much of a hassle.

And then other nights it’s the total opposite. I’ll be up late and suddenly I’m tearing through takeout or whatever snacks are around after midnight, like I can’t really get a handle on it. It feels weird because it’s not just “oh, I skipped lunch” or “I had a big dinner,” it’s been swinging back and forth enough that I started getting worried something’s off.

Not really, no. I mean, I guess I notice if my clothes feel different or if I look tired in the mirror, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there worrying about my weight or picking apart my body or anything like that.

It’s more like the eating itself feels off, if that makes sense. Like I’ll realize later that I didn’t eat, or I’ll eat a bunch at night, and then I think, “Why did I do that?” But it’s not because I’m trying to lose weight or because I’m unhappy with how I look. Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I’d call it “down” all the time exactly, but I’ve definitely lost interest in stuff I normally care about, and that’s been bothering me.

Like, I used to play soccer on weekends pretty regularly, and usually I’d look forward to it all week, even if work was stressful. Lately I’ll get the invite or see the group chat going and I just… don’t care enough to go. Same with cooking for my girlfriend — I used to actually enjoy planning something, trying a recipe, making it kind of a nice thing for us. Now it feels like this huge effort, and I end up not wanting to do it.

Even small things, like checking out little galleries or helping the newer sales guys at work — I used to get something out of that. It made me feel useful, I guess. But lately I just can’t make myself care about any of it. It’s like the interest is there in my head as something I remember having, but I don’t really feel it.

I’m not totally sure I’d say depressed most of the day, nearly every day. That sounds… stronger than what I can confidently say, I guess.

There have definitely been stretches where I feel kind of flat or heavy, like I’m just going through the motions and everything takes more effort than it should. It’s like the interest is there in my head as something I remember having, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure I’d say depressed most of the day, nearly every day. Yeah… I think there have been days like that. It’s hard for me to count them cleanly, because it hasn’t been like one big obvious crash where I can point to a date and say, “That’s when it started.” It’s more like this low feeling and not caring about things has been hanging around for, I don’t know, about four months now.

Some days it’s kind of in the background, like I can still get through work and talk to people, but I feel dull or weighed down most of the day. Other days I don’t notice it until later, when I realize I barely cared about anything I did. The guilt part comes in too, especially when I think about stuff I’m letting slide or how I’m acting with people.

It got harder to ignore after the trouble at work. Before that I could tell myself I was just tired or stressed, but after that, it was like… okay, this has been going on a while, and it’s not just a couple bad days. I wouldn’t say every single day is like that, but it’s been happening often enough over these past few months that it feels like a pattern now.

Yeah, I have noticed that. It’s not even like I’m running around all day or doing something that should wipe me out. Sometimes I’ll actually lie down for hours, thinking if I just rest enough I’ll feel normal again, but then the next day my body still feels drained.

I’ll get there and feel like I already used up a bunch of energy just getting myself and the cases inside. It’s frustrating because it makes me feel kind of weak or lazy, even though I know it’s not that I’ve been doing anything especially physical.

It’s hard for me to count them cleanly, because it hasn’t been like one big obvious crash where I can point to a date and say, “That’s when it started.” It’s more like this low feeling and not caring about things has been hanging around for, I don’t know, about four months now.\n\nSome days it’s kind of in the background, like I can still get through work and talk to people, but I feel dull or weighed down most of the day. Other days I don’t notice it until later, when I realize I barely cared about anything I did. The guilt part comes in too, especially when I think about stuff I’m letting slide or how I’m acting with people.\n\nIt got harder to ignore after the trouble at work. Before that I could tell myself I was just tired or stressed, but after that, it was like… okay, this has been going on a while, and it’s not just a couple bad days. I wouldn’t say every single day is like that, but it’s been happening often enough over these past few months that it feels like a pattern now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling fatigued or having a loss of energy nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing much physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have noticed that. It’s not even like I’m running around all day or doing something that should wipe me out. I’ll get there and feel like I already used up a bunch of energy just getting myself and the cases inside. It’s frustrating because it makes me feel kind of weak or lazy, even though I know it’s not that I’ve been doing anything especially physical.\n\n[Last Question]\nWould you say that there have been times when you felt down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t have it marked on a calendar or anything, but the low mood and the guilt and that feeling of just not caring about things have been around for about four months now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… that’s actually one of the things that kind of scared me into coming in, honestly. My eating has been really all over the place, and it doesn’t feel like a normal “I’m busy so I skipped lunch” kind of thing.

Some days I’ll realize I basically forgot meals completely, like I’ll get to late afternoon or evening and think, wait, did I actually eat anything today? And then other nights it flips the other way, where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m trying to make up for the whole day or something. It feels messy and not like me.

I’m not dieting or trying to lose weight or anything like that. It’s more like I either don’t care enough to eat, or I suddenly want everything at once really late at night. I don’t know if my weight has changed a lot because I haven’t been tracking it closely, but the appetite part has definitely been noticeable.

carrying sample cases into a client meeting takes more out of me than it should.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep replaying every mistake with those Providence accounts and thinking I blew my whole future, like I'm useless and everybody at work can see it.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I've had these thoughts where I wish I could just not wake up for a while, but I haven't made a plan to hurt myself.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of manifestation: There were days I had so much energy that I was rewriting decks, calling customers, mapping new routes, and trying to fix every account at once instead of slowing down.\n- key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: People have been telling me I talk too fast in meetings, and I notice I keep jumping in because silence feels like I'm losing the room.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts feel like they're stacked on top of each other, going from sales ideas to my boss to money to some totally different pitch before I can finish the first one.\n- key: S039\n  name: I’m still showing up, but work and home both feel like they’re slipping out of my control.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 month\n  manifestation: This whole episode has been going on since the fight with my regional manager about a month ago.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: The low mood, guilt, and not caring about things have been around for about four months, and they got harder to ignore after the trouble at work.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / sales_representative_wholesale_or_manufacturing / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling fatigued or having a loss of energy nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing much physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have noticed that. It’s not even like I’m running around all day or doing something that should wipe me out. And then other nights it flips the other way, where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m trying to make up for the whole day or something. I don’t know if my weight has changed a lot because I haven’t been tracking it closely, but the appetite part has definitely been noticeable.\n\n[Last Question]\nWould you say that there were times when you felt down or low nearly every day, for most of the day, for at least two weeks straight?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I would say so. I know I kind of already said this, but it’s been going on long enough that it doesn’t feel like just a rough few days or a bad week. The low mood, the guilt, and this not-caring feeling have been hanging around for about four months now.

And after the trouble at work, it got harder to ignore. Before that I think I was trying to explain it away, like maybe I was just stressed or tired, but after that it felt more obvious that something had been building for a while. So yeah, at least two weeks straight, definitely.

Not even on purpose, I just forget meals completely, or I’ll feel like stopping to eat is too much of a hassle.\n\nAnd then other nights it’s the total opposite. I’ll be up late and suddenly I’m tearing through takeout or whatever snacks are around after midnight, like I can’t really get a handle on it. I mean, I guess I notice if my clothes feel different or if I look tired in the mirror, but I wouldn’t say I’m sitting there worrying about my weight or picking apart my body or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like the eating itself feels off, if that makes sense. Like I’ll realize later that I didn’t eat, or I’ll eat a bunch at night, and then I think, “Why did I do that?” But it’s not because I’m trying to lose weight or because I’m unhappy with how I look. At least I don’t think so. It doesn’t feel like a body image thing to me.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you noticed yourself feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I’d call it “down” all the time exactly, but I’ve definitely lost interest in stuff I normally care about, and that’s been bothering me.\n\nLike, I used to play soccer on weekends pretty regularly, and usually I’d look forward to it all week, even if work was stressful. Lately I’ll get the invite or see the group chat going and I just… don’t care enough to go. Same with cooking for my girlfriend — I used to actually enjoy planning something, trying a recipe, making it kind of a nice thing for us. Now it feels like this huge effort, and I end up not wanting to do it.\n\nEven small things, like checking out little galleries or helping the newer sales guys at work — I used to get something out of that. It made me feel useful, I guess. But lately I just can’t make myself care about any of it. It’s like the interest is there in my head as something I remember having, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure I’d say depressed most of the day, nearly every day. Other days I don’t notice it until later, when I realize I barely cared about anything I did. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, my sleep has been really off. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I know I should be able to sleep, but my mind just won’t stop running through work stuff — pitches, what I should say to a client, how I could word something better, replaying conversations, thinking through orders or calls or whatever. It’s like I’m lying there physically tired but mentally still at the office.

Or I do fall asleep, but then I wake up way before my alarm, sometimes hours early, and I feel weirdly wired. Not rested, exactly — I can tell I barely slept — but my body acts like it’s time to get up and go. And then I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling, or I’ll start thinking again, and once that starts it’s basically over. I don’t usually just roll over and go back to sleep easily.

So yeah, it’s not like one bad night here and there. It’s been most nights lately, and it’s starting to make me feel kind of run down and on edge because I know I’m not getting normal sleep.

Some days it’s more noticeable, and other days I’m distracted enough by work or whatever that I don’t really label it that way.\n\nSo… yeah, I’ve felt off and low at times, but I’m not sure it’s been most of the day every day.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days where you felt down or low for most of the day, and if so, how often that happens?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there have been days like that. It’s hard for me to count them cleanly, because it hasn’t been like one big obvious crash where I can point to a date and say, “That’s when it started.” It’s more like this low feeling and not caring about things has been hanging around for, I don’t know, about four months now.\n\nSome days it’s kind of in the background, like I can still get through work and talk to people, but I feel dull or weighed down most of the day. Other days I don’t notice it until later, when I realize I barely cared about anything I did. The guilt part comes in too, especially when I think about stuff I’m letting slide or how I’m acting with people.\n\nIt got harder to ignore after the trouble at work. Before that I could tell myself I was just tired or stressed, but after that, it was like… okay, this has been going on a while, and it’s not just a couple bad days. I wouldn’t say every single day is like that, but it’s been happening often enough over these past few months that it feels like a pattern now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling fatigued or having a loss of energy nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing much physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have noticed that. It’s not even like I’m running around all day or doing something that should wipe me out. Sometimes I’ll actually lie down for hours, thinking if I just rest enough I’ll feel normal again, but then the next day my body still feels drained.\n\nLike with work, carrying sample cases into a client meeting shouldn’t feel like some huge thing. I’ve done that forever, it’s part of the job, but lately it takes more out of me than it should. I’ll get there and feel like I already used up a bunch of energy just getting myself and the cases inside. It’s frustrating because it makes me feel kind of weak or lazy, even though I know it’s not that I’ve been doing anything especially physical.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there have been times when you felt down or low for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t have it marked on a calendar or anything, but the low mood and the guilt and that feeling of just not caring about things have been around for about four months now.\n\nIt hasn’t been the exact same every day, but there have definitely been stretches where it felt like most days were like that, where I’d go through the whole day with this heavy, dull feeling. And after the trouble at work, it got a lot harder to ignore, because before that I kept telling myself it was just stress or I was tired or whatever. But looking back, yeah, I’d say there were at least a couple weeks in a row where it was there nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently that don’t seem to be related to anything else like a medical issue or intentional dieting?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually one of the things that kind of scared me into coming in, honestly. My eating has been really all over the place, and it doesn’t feel like a normal “I’m busy so I skipped lunch” kind of thing.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I basically forgot meals completely, like I’ll get to late afternoon or evening and think, wait, did I actually eat anything today? And then other nights it flips the other way, where it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout or snacks like I’m trying to make up for the whole day or something. It feels messy and not like me.\n\nI’m not dieting or trying to lose weight or anything like that. It’s more like I either don’t care enough to eat, or I suddenly want everything at once really late at night. I don’t know if my weight has changed a lot because I haven’t been tracking it closely, but the appetite part has definitely been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there were times when you felt down or low nearly every day, for most of the day, for at least two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I would say so. I know I kind of already said this, but it’s been going on long enough that it doesn’t feel like just a rough few days or a bad week. The low mood, the guilt, and this not-caring feeling have been hanging around for about four months now.\n\nIt wasn’t like every single minute was exactly the same, but there were definitely stretches where I’d wake up and it was just… there, most of the day, almost every day. And after the trouble at work, it got harder to ignore. Before that I think I was trying to explain it away, like maybe I was just stressed or tired, but after that it felt more obvious that something had been building for a while. So yeah, at least two weeks straight, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s been happening a lot, especially at work. I’ll be in the middle of entering an order, something I’ve done a million times, and then my phone buzzes, or I hear a truck outside, or somebody walks past the office, and it’s like my brain just gets yanked completely away from what I was doing.\n\nAnd then when I come back to it, I’ll realize I missed some basic detail I normally would catch, like a number or part of the order or something that should be obvious. It’s frustrating because it’s not like I don’t know how to do the job. I do. But lately even small distractions feel way bigger than they should, and it takes me longer to get back on track.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up in the middle of the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been really off. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I know I should be able to sleep, but my mind just won’t stop running through work stuff — pitches, what I should say to a client, how I could word something better, replaying conversations, thinking through orders or calls or whatever. It’s like I’m lying there physically tired but mentally still at the office.\n\nOr I do fall asleep, but then I wake up way before my alarm, sometimes hours early, and I feel weirdly wired. Not rested, exactly — I can tell I barely slept — but my body acts like it’s time to get up and go. And then I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling, or I’ll start thinking again, and once that starts it’s basically over. I don’t usually just roll over and go back to sleep easily.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like one bad night here and there. It’s been most nights lately, and it’s starting to make me feel kind of run down and on edge because I know I’m not getting normal sleep.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The low mood, the guilt, and this not-caring feeling have been hanging around for about four months now.\n\nIt wasn’t like every single minute was exactly the same, but there were definitely stretches where I’d wake up and it was just… there, most of the day, almost every day. Before that I think I was trying to explain it away, like maybe I was just stressed or tired, but after that it felt more obvious that something had been building for a while. So yeah, at least two weeks straight, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s been happening a lot, especially at work. I’ll be in the middle of entering an order, something I’ve done a million times, and then my phone buzzes, or I hear a truck outside, or somebody walks past the office, and it’s like my brain just gets yanked completely away from what I was doing.\n\nAnd then when I come back to it, I’ll realize I missed some basic detail I normally would catch, like a number or part of the order or something that should be obvious. It’s frustrating because it’s not like I don’t know how to do the job. I do. But lately even small distractions feel way bigger than they should, and it takes me longer to get back on track.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up in the middle of the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been really off. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I know I should be able to sleep, but my mind just won’t stop running through work stuff — pitches, what I should say to a client, how I could word something better, replaying conversations, thinking through orders or calls or whatever. It’s like I’m lying there physically tired but mentally still at the office.\n\nOr I do fall asleep, but then I wake up way before my alarm, sometimes hours early, and I feel weirdly wired. Not rested, exactly — I can tell I barely slept — but my body acts like it’s time to get up and go. And then I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling, or I’ll start thinking again, and once that starts it’s basically over. I don’t usually just roll over and go back to sleep easily.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like one bad night here and there. It’s been most nights lately, and it’s starting to make me feel kind of run down and on edge because I know I’m not getting normal sleep.\n\n[Last Question]\nLately, have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or that you just can't sit still and feel restless much of the time?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / sales_representative_wholesale_or_manufacturing / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Psychomotor_Changes\nmanifestation: \"At work I catch myself pacing around my car before appointments and tapping my hands on the steering wheel, but then at home I can sit on the couch and feel like even getting up takes forever.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think both, kind of, which sounds weird. It depends where I am.

Like at work, before an appointment, I’ll get there early and instead of just sitting in the car like a normal person, I’ll be out walking around it or pacing near the parking lot, checking my phone, tapping on the steering wheel if I’m still inside. My hands just keep doing something. I’ll drum my fingers or keep shifting in the seat, and I don’t always notice until I’m already doing it. It feels like there’s this anxious energy in me and I can’t just be still while I’m waiting.

But then when I’m home, it’s almost the opposite. I can sit on the couch and feel like getting up is this huge thing, like my body is moving through mud or something. I’ll think, “I should get up,” and then I just sit there way longer than I mean to, because even standing up feels like it takes more effort than it should. So it’s not one simple thing, I guess. At work I’m restless and fidgety, and at home I feel slowed down.

I’ll be up late and suddenly I’m tearing through takeout or whatever snacks are around after midnight, like I can’t really get a handle on it. Other days I don’t notice it until later, when I realize I barely cared about anything I did. And after the trouble at work, it got a lot harder to ignore, because before that I kept telling myself it was just stress or I was tired or whatever. But looking back, yeah, I’d say there were at least a couple weeks in a row where it was there nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently that don’t seem to be related to anything else like a medical issue or intentional dieting?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually one of the things that kind of scared me into coming in, honestly. Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s actually one of the things that bothers me the most, because it’s not like I never had stuff I cared about. I did. I used to play soccer on weekends, and that was kind of my thing — not professionally or anything, just with guys I knew, but I looked forward to it all week. Even if work was annoying, I’d think, okay, at least Saturday I’ll get out there, run around, joke with everyone. Lately, I just… I don’t care enough to go. I’ll see the messages about a game and just ignore them or tell myself I’ll decide later, and then I don’t.

And cooking too. I used to cook for my girlfriend, like actually put effort into it — look up recipes, get ingredients, make it kind of a nice thing. Now even thinking about doing that feels like this chore I can’t get myself to start. It’s not that I hate it. It’s more like there’s no pull toward it anymore.

Same with going to small galleries. We used to check out little local art places sometimes, and I liked that, even if I didn’t always understand everything. It felt interesting. Now if she brings it up, I’m just kind of flat about it, like, “Yeah, maybe,” but inside I’m already hoping we don’t go.

Even at work, I used to like helping the newer sales guys, showing them how to handle certain calls or avoid mistakes I made when I started. That used to make me feel useful. Lately, I just can’t make myself care about that either. I’ll still answer if someone asks me something, but I’m not really invested the way I used to be. It’s like all those things are still there, but whatever part of me used to enjoy them is just not really showing up.

It feels like there’s this anxious energy in me and I can’t just be still while I’m waiting.\n\nBut then when I’m home, it’s almost the opposite. I can sit on the couch and feel like getting up is this huge thing, like my body is moving through mud or something. I’ll think, “I should get up,” and then I just sit there way longer than I mean to, because even standing up feels like it takes more effort than it should. So it’s not one simple thing, I guess. At work I’m restless and fidgety, and at home I feel slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure or interest in things you used to really enjoy, even things that were important to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s actually one of the things that bothers me the most, because it’s not like I never had stuff I cared about. I did. I used to play soccer on weekends, and that was kind of my thing — not professionally or anything, just with guys I knew, but I looked forward to it all week. Even if work was annoying, I’d think, okay, at least Saturday I’ll get out there, run around, joke with everyone. Lately, I just… I don’t care enough to go. I’ll see the messages about a game and just ignore them or tell myself I’ll decide later, and then I don’t.\n\nAnd cooking too. I used to cook for my girlfriend, like actually put effort into it — look up recipes, get ingredients, make it kind of a nice thing. Now even thinking about doing that feels like this chore I can’t get myself to start. It’s not that I hate it. It’s more like there’s no pull toward it anymore.\n\nSame with going to small galleries. We used to check out little local art places sometimes, and I liked that, even if I didn’t always understand everything. It felt interesting. Now if she brings it up, I’m just kind of flat about it, like, “Yeah, maybe,” but inside I’m already hoping we don’t go.\n\nEven at work, I used to like helping the newer sales guys, showing them how to handle certain calls or avoid mistakes I made when I started. That used to make me feel useful. Lately, I just can’t make myself care about that either. I’ll still answer if someone asks me something, but I’m not really invested the way I used to be. It’s like all those things are still there, but whatever part of me used to enjoy them is just not really showing up.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave there been periods where you felt down or low nearly every day, for most of the day, that lasted for at least two weeks in a row?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / sales_representative_wholesale_or_manufacturing / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressive Episode Duration\nmanifestation: \"The low mood, guilt, and not caring about things have been around for about four months, and they got harder to ignore after the trouble at work.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. Not just a few bad days, if that’s what you mean. It’s been more like this low feeling hanging around almost all the time for… I’d say about four months now.

At first I kind of told myself it was just stress or that I was tired, and I could push through it, because I’ve had rough weeks before. But this didn’t really clear up. The not caring about things, feeling guilty about stuff, just feeling kind of weighed down — that’s been there most days. Maybe some parts of the day are a little better if I’m distracted, but it’s not like I wake up and feel normal for very long.

And it got a lot harder to ignore after the trouble at work. Before that I could sort of explain it away, like, “I’m just busy,” or “I’m just in a funk.” But after that happened, it felt like it was right in my face. So yeah, definitely longer than two weeks. More like months.

I don’t know if my weight has changed a lot because I haven’t been tracking it closely, but the appetite part has definitely been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there were times when you felt down or low nearly every day, for most of the day, for at least two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I would say so. I know I kind of already said this, but it’s been going on long enough that it doesn’t feel like just a rough few days or a bad week. The low mood, the guilt, and this not-caring feeling have been hanging around for about four months now.\n\nIt wasn’t like every single minute was exactly the same, but there were definitely stretches where I’d wake up and it was just… there, most of the day, almost every day. And after the trouble at work, it got harder to ignore. Before that I think I was trying to explain it away, like maybe I was just stressed or tired, but after that it felt more obvious that something had been building for a while. So yeah, at least two weeks straight, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble focusing, making decisions, or thinking clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. That’s been happening a lot, especially at work. I’ll be in the middle of entering an order, something I’ve done a million times, and then my phone buzzes, or I hear a truck outside, or somebody walks past the office, and it’s like my brain just gets yanked completely away from what I was doing.\n\nAnd then when I come back to it, I’ll realize I missed some basic detail I normally would catch, like a number or part of the order or something that should be obvious. It’s frustrating because it’s not like I don’t know how to do the job. I do. But lately even small distractions feel way bigger than they should, and it takes me longer to get back on track.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up in the middle of the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been really off. Most nights it’s one of two things. Either I get into bed and I’m exhausted, like I know I should be able to sleep, but my mind just won’t stop running through work stuff — pitches, what I should say to a client, how I could word something better, replaying conversations, thinking through orders or calls or whatever. It’s like I’m lying there physically tired but mentally still at the office.\n\nOr I do fall asleep, but then I wake up way before my alarm, sometimes hours early, and I feel weirdly wired. Not rested, exactly — I can tell I barely slept — but my body acts like it’s time to get up and go. And then I’ll just lie there staring at the ceiling, or I’ll start thinking again, and once that starts it’s basically over. I don’t usually just roll over and go back to sleep easily.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not like one bad night here and there. It’s been most nights lately, and it’s starting to make me feel kind of run down and on edge because I know I’m not getting normal sleep.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you noticed that you're moving more slowly than usual, or that you just can't sit still and feel restless much of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think both, kind of, which sounds weird. It depends where I am.\n\nLike at work, before an appointment, I’ll get there early and instead of just sitting in the car like a normal person, I’ll be out walking around it or pacing near the parking lot, checking my phone, tapping on the steering wheel if I’m still inside. My hands just keep doing something. I’ll drum my fingers or keep shifting in the seat, and I don’t always notice until I’m already doing it. It feels like there’s this anxious energy in me and I can’t just be still while I’m waiting.\n\nBut then when I’m home, it’s almost the opposite. I can sit on the couch and feel like getting up is this huge thing, like my body is moving through mud or something. I’ll think, “I should get up,” and then I just sit there way longer than I mean to, because even standing up feels like it takes more effort than it should. So it’s not one simple thing, I guess. At work I’m restless and fidgety, and at home I feel slowed down.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you no longer take pleasure or interest in things you used to really enjoy, even things that were important to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have noticed that. It’s actually one of the things that bothers me the most, because it’s not like I never had stuff I cared about. I did. I used to play soccer on weekends, and that was kind of my thing — not professionally or anything, just with guys I knew, but I looked forward to it all week. Even if work was annoying, I’d think, okay, at least Saturday I’ll get out there, run around, joke with everyone. Lately, I just… I don’t care enough to go. I’ll see the messages about a game and just ignore them or tell myself I’ll decide later, and then I don’t.\n\nAnd cooking too. I used to cook for my girlfriend, like actually put effort into it — look up recipes, get ingredients, make it kind of a nice thing. Now even thinking about doing that feels like this chore I can’t get myself to start. It’s not that I hate it. It’s more like there’s no pull toward it anymore.\n\nSame with going to small galleries. We used to check out little local art places sometimes, and I liked that, even if I didn’t always understand everything. It felt interesting. Now if she brings it up, I’m just kind of flat about it, like, “Yeah, maybe,” but inside I’m already hoping we don’t go.\n\nEven at work, I used to like helping the newer sales guys, showing them how to handle certain calls or avoid mistakes I made when I started. That used to make me feel useful. Lately, I just can’t make myself care about that either. I’ll still answer if someone asks me something, but I’m not really invested the way I used to be. It’s like all those things are still there, but whatever part of me used to enjoy them is just not really showing up.\n\nDoctor: Have there been periods where you felt down or low nearly every day, for most of the day, that lasted for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not just a few bad days, if that’s what you mean. It’s been more like this low feeling hanging around almost all the time for… I’d say about four months now.\n\nAt first I kind of told myself it was just stress or that I was tired, and I could push through it, because I’ve had rough weeks before. But this didn’t really clear up. The not caring about things, feeling guilty about stuff, just feeling kind of weighed down — that’s been there most days. Maybe some parts of the day are a little better if I’m distracted, but it’s not like I wake up and feel normal for very long.\n\nAnd it got a lot harder to ignore after the trouble at work. Before that I could sort of explain it away, like, “I’m just busy,” or “I’m just in a funk.” But after that happened, it felt like it was right in my face. So yeah, definitely longer than two weeks. More like months."
